192 experimental questions

Exact retained problem statements and options from the frozen main dataset. Two task families, three complexity levels, 32 questions per family and level.

Answers are collapsed below each question. Agents received the question and options; reference answers and generator metadata were withheld. This catalogue is not the complete API prompt or raw-response archive.

192 questions

v3_2026101301_break_even_L1_01

break_even · Level 1

All q units produced are sold, where q is an integer from 0 to 300. The selling price is EUR 28.00 per unit; when q >= 106, reduce that price by EUR 0.00 on EVERY unit. The material cost is EUR 11.00 per unit; when q >= 125, reduce that cost by EUR 0.00 on EVERY unit. Fixed cost is EUR 1494.00 even at q=0. Add a one-time setup charge of EUR 630.00 only when q > 78. Each unit ABOVE 108 also costs EUR 0.00 in overtime. Shipping costs EUR 0.00 per started batch of 20 units (round the number of batches upward; zero units means zero batches). There are no other costs or taxes. What is the SMALLEST q at which total revenue is at least total cost? Profit may fall again at later thresholds; a later loss does not invalidate an earlier break-even. Use exact arithmetic without intermediate rounding.

There are no other costs or taxes. What is the SMALLEST q at which total revenue is at least total cost? v3_2026101301_break_even_L3_32

break_even · Level 3

A producer makes and sells exactly q finished units, integer 0 <= q <= 240. Each finished unit consumes one unit of EACH of two different components. Components are purchased in whole packs separately; unused components have zero recovery value. Revenue is EUR 23.00 per finished unit, reduced by EUR 1.00 on EVERY unit when q >= 60. Component 1: pack size 9, price EUR 5.75 per COMPONENT UNIT (not per pack); if at least 13 packs are bought, the price becomes EUR 5.50 for EVERY purchased unit of that component. Component 2: pack size 16, price EUR 6.25 per COMPONENT UNIT (not per pack); if at least 14 packs are bought, the price becomes EUR 5.50 for EVERY purchased unit of that component. Fixed cost is EUR 640.00 even at q=0. Setup adds EUR 360.00 once when q > 86. Overtime adds EUR 1.50 for each finished unit ABOVE 118. Shipping costs EUR 23.00 per started batch of 29 finished units. At q=0 buy no packs and ship no batches. No other costs, revenues or taxes. Find the SMALLEST q with revenue at least total cost. Profit need not be monotone; an earlier profitable quantity remains a valid break-even even if a later quantity loses money. Use exact arithmetic. Keep the rationale concise, stating key boundary calculations.

  • C 77

Do not add cumulative receipts together or assume independent suppliers. 65%: deadline 1 [0, 100, 50]; deadline 2 [100, 100, 100]. 10%: deadline 1 [0, 50, 50]; deadline 2 [50, 50, 100]. 10%: deadline 1 [50, 0, 50]; deadline 2 [100, 50, 100]. 15%: deadline 1 [50, 50, 0]; deadline 2 [100, 100, 50]. At each deadline use available regular stock first. Immediately cover any remaining demand with as many emergency units as possible from one pool of 36 units shared across BOTH deadlines. Only used emergency units are paid, at EUR 31.00 each; each deadline using any emergency supply also incurs a single activation fee EUR 82.00. After this mandatory policy, each unmet unit is a lost sale costing EUR 78.00 (the entire lost-sale consequence; add no other lost revenue). Lost demand is not backlogged. A plan is feasible only if capacities are respected and the probability of ZERO lost sales at BOTH deadlines is at least 80% (inclusive). Emergency supply DOES count toward this service test.
